| [agent](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/agent "agent") | [agent](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/agent "agent") | [Prevent shared instruction providers from delegating to themselves (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/9d53dd91b5b5411dbfa8366fe7fd352ab3b1ddec "Prevent shared instruction providers from delegating to themselves (#50093)  ## Why  Reloading an unloaded root from a surviving child can supply the shared instruction wrapper already registered for that root. Replacing its underlying provider with that same wrapper makes instruction loading recurse into itself.  ## What changed  Reuse the existing wrapper without replacing its provider when both are the same `Arc`. Continue to accept distinct replacement providers.  ## Testing  Extend regression coverage to reload the root from a surviving child and verify that shared instructions still update. Check wrapper reuse, replacement with a distinct shared provider, and freezing the shared snapshot when switching to a provider that does not share with subagents.  GitOrigin-RevId: 7b6b2f9031f206e203f6fa14e1478a36b305ec39") [#…](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/50093) | yesterdayOct 1, 2026 |
| [apps](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/apps "apps") | [apps](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/apps "apps") | [Make Apps guidance react to MCP availability (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/3c2bfe7c5d05c33b4d961d692fac9bb888867150 "Make Apps guidance react to MCP availability (#30226)  ## Why  Generic Apps guidance is emitted only while building static initial context. If the Apps MCP is unavailable then and recovers later in the same turn, its tools can become usable without the model receiving the guidance for using them.  ## What  - move generic Apps guidance into a persisted `apps_instructions` World State section - derive availability from the request's MCP runtime while preserving the existing feature, auth, orchestrator-MCP, and config gates - recognize legacy and retained Apps fragments so resume and compaction do not duplicate guidance - register Apps guidance as rollback-trimmable context - remove the old static injection path and its now-unused connector helper - keep tool construction on its existing independent `list_all_tools()` read rather than adding a request-wide cache; a reconnect between the two reads can differ for one request and reconciles on the next request  Apps and plugin guidance now render after the remaining static host-skills block, with Apps before Plugins.  ## Testing  - `just fmt` - `just test -p codex-core apps_instructions` - `just test -p codex-core apps_guidance_appears_after_background_recovery_within_a_turn` - `just test -p codex-core drop_last_n_user_turns_trims_context_updates_above_rolled_back_turn`") [#30226](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/30226) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/3c2bfe7c5d05c33b4d961d692fac9bb888867150 "Make Apps guidance react to MCP availability (#30226)  ## Why  Generic Apps guidance is emitted only while building static initial context. If the Apps MCP is unavailable then and recovers later in the same turn, its tools can become usable without the model receiving the guidance for using them.  ## What  - move generic Apps guidance into a persisted `apps_instructions` World State section - derive availability from the request's MCP runtime while preserving the existing feature, auth, orchestrator-MCP, and config gates - recognize legacy and retained Apps fragments so resume and compaction do not duplicate guidance - register Apps guidance as rollback-trimmable context - remove the old static injection path and its now-unused connector helper - keep tool construction on its existing independent `list_all_tools()` read rather than adding a request-wide cache; a reconnect between the two reads can differ for one request and reconciles on the next request  Apps and plugin guidance now render after the remaining static host-skills block, with Apps before Plugins.  ## Testing  - `just fmt` - `just test -p codex-core apps_instructions` - `just test -p codex-core apps_guidance_appears_after_background_recovery_within_a_turn` - `just test -p codex-core drop_last_n_user_turns_trims_context_updates_above_rolled_back_turn`") | 3 months agoJul 6, 2026 |
| [config](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/config "config") | [config](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/config "config") | [Make transcript mouse scroll speed configurable (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/4dd51f4a5f2037f8aa322fe7807315e6530a4ec8 "Make transcript mouse scroll speed configurable (#50209)  ## What changed  Add `tui.mouse_scroll_speed` as a finite positive multiplier for transcript mouse wheel scrolling, including transcript overlays and session previews. Change the default from three rows to one row per wheel event; set the multiplier to `3.0` to retain the previous speed.  Fractional values accumulate movement across events and discard the remainder when direction reverses. For example, `0.5` scrolls one row every two events. Following the latest output pauses only when a whole row moves.  ## Testing  Add tests for configuration validation, integer and fractional scroll speeds, direction changes, decimal accumulation, and following behavior. Extend local settings coverage to include a command-line override for the new setting.  GitOrigin-RevId: d355e246fd943415f0ce5a6affe849f77f3615e6") [#50209](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/50209) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/4dd51f4a5f2037f8aa322fe7807315e6530a4ec8 "Make transcript mouse scroll speed configurable (#50209)  ## What changed  Add `tui.mouse_scroll_speed` as a finite positive multiplier for transcript mouse wheel scrolling, including transcript overlays and session previews. | [context](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/context "context") | [context](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/context "context") | [Send incremental tool catalog updates in Responses Lite (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/6326163b9abd7802c0e57be4e326e5f898bbba75 "Send incremental tool catalog updates in Responses Lite (#50540)  ## What changed  When `IncrementalTools` is enabled for Responses Lite, track tool definitions in world state and conversation history. Send the initial catalog once, then append only added or changed definitions while preserving earlier request history. Emit developer notices for removed tools and namespace instruction changes.  Use history-supplied definitions for normal requests and remote compaction, and omit empty `additional_tools` prefixes. Keep the existing tool delivery behavior for the Responses API and when the feature is disabled.  ## Testing  Add unit and snapshot coverage for catalog and namespace transitions, empty catalogs, and stable hashing across JSON object key order. Add a request-history scenario covering unchanged tools, a model switch that changes one definition, persisted catalog hashes, and Responses API behavior. | [exec\_policy](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/exec_policy "exec_policy") | [exec\_policy](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/exec_policy "exec_policy") | [Apply remote platform semantics to exec safety checks (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/1f4c47343a1bff2d8cddc429c5d39503fb5a6c30 "Apply remote platform semantics to exec safety checks (#42113)  ## Why  Unified exec can run commands on an OS that differs from the Codex host. Using host path and shell semantics for approval checks can therefore miss dangerous Windows and PowerShell commands sent to a remote executor.  ## What changed  - Thread the executor platform through command parsing, executable-name   normalization, and dangerous-command classification. Legacy executors fall   back to the host platform, while unknown reported platforms use Windows rules   conservatively. - Bound model-facing `exec_command` rejection messages and avoid echoing the   rejected command into the error. - Keep deterministic process IDs reserved after release so rejected test   commands cannot reuse an earlier ID.  ## Testing  - Add coverage that a long dangerous PowerShell command targeting a remote   Windows executor is rejected with a bounded response. - Verify deterministic process IDs advance after release.  GitOrigin-RevId: a26b4a63ea3d6df7140032b88b3e7aec0b85b948") [#42113](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/42113) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/1f4c47343a1bff2d8cddc429c5d39503fb5a6c30 "Apply remote platform semantics to exec safety checks (#42113)  ## Why  Unified exec can run commands on an OS that differs from the Codex host. Using host path and shell semantics for approval checks can therefore miss dangerous Windows and PowerShell commands sent to a remote executor.  ## What changed  - Thread the executor platform through command parsing, executable-name   normalization, and dangerous-command classification. | [mcp\_tool\_call](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/mcp_tool_call "mcp_tool_call") | [mcp\_tool\_call](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/mcp_tool_call "mcp_tool_call") | [Add opt-in conversation history retrieval to Guardian reviews (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/41ed72c32b4980cd7919e1c2a45ecb1f96c5911a "Add opt-in conversation history retrieval to Guardian reviews (#49036)  ## Why  The transcript supplied to Guardian may omit earlier user instructions, restrictions, or revoked permissions. History retrieval lets the reviewer check relevant authorization before approving actions with side effects.  ## What changed  - Add the disabled-by-default `guardian_conversation_history_tools` feature. With Apps enabled, expose `user_message.search_messages` and `user_message.read_messages` through the parent's live Apps connection and conversation identity. - Recheck the parent's current app and tool policy on each call, rejecting disabled tools and calls requiring approval. - Add retrieval instructions that distinguish user authorization from assistant context and account for later revocations and incomplete results. Allow overrides through `auto_review.experimental_conversation_history_prompt`. - Limit history responses to an estimated 4,000 tokens by default, configurable through `auto_review.conversation_history_max_output_tokens`, while preserving stricter parent and reviewer limits. - Allow explicit extension registries for isolated sessions and prevent those sessions from inheriting the implicit Apps connection.  ## Testing  Add scenarios covering history tool exposure, parent connection and identity reuse, custom prompts, output truncation, stricter reviewer budgets, and live permission changes on a reused reviewer. Extend configuration coverage for blank prompt overrides.  GitOrigin-RevId: 055a69b113c8548bd58e7d5618ea026ba6d1ffb1") [#49036](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/49036) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/41ed72c32b4980cd7919e1c2a45ecb1f96c5911a "Add opt-in conversation history retrieval to Guardian reviews (#49036)  ## Why  The transcript supplied to Guardian may omit earlier user instructions, restrictions, or revoked permissions. History retrieval lets the reviewer check relevant authorization before approving actions with side effects.  ## What changed  - Add the disabled-by-default `guardian_conversation_history_tools` feature. With Apps enabled, expose `user_message.search_messages` and `user_message.read_messages` through the parent's live Apps connection and conversation identity. - Recheck the parent's current app and tool policy on each call, rejecting disabled tools and calls requiring approval. - Add retrieval instructions that distinguish user authorization from assistant context and account for later revocations and incomplete results. Allow overrides through `auto_review.experimental_conversation_history_prompt`. - Limit history responses to an estimated 4,000 tokens by default, configurable through `auto_review.conversation_history_max_output_tokens`, while preserving stricter parent and reviewer limits. - Allow explicit extension registries for isolated sessions and prevent those sessions from inheriting the implicit Apps connection.  ## Testing  Add scenarios covering history tool exposure, parent connection and identity reuse, custom prompts, output truncation, stricter reviewer budgets, and live permission changes on a reused reviewer. Extend configuration coverage for blank prompt overrides.  GitOrigin-RevId: 055a69b113c8548bd58e7d5618ea026ba6d1ffb1") | 5 days agoSep 28, 2026 |
| [plugins](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/plugins "plugins") | [plugins](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/plugins "plugins") | [Attribute command and plugin analytics to the invoking model (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/4d8eca1ff34ff9da717323a7b0a5d0b5ebcf3bb6 "Attribute command and plugin analytics to the invoking model (#45445)  ## Why  Command execution and plugin measurement events lack model and reasoning-effort labels. Attribution needs to reflect the step that invoked the command, even when model settings change before a background process finishes.  ## What changed  - Add `model_slug` and `reasoning_effort` to command execution and plugin measurement analytics. - Capture model context from resolved step settings and carry it through execution, approval, Guardian review, and plugin metrics collection. - Preserve the first command-start model context when subsequent start notifications arrive. - Keep the carried context out of serialized protocol items and generated schemas.  ## Testing  Extend analytics tests to cover model switches before invocation and during background execution, default reasoning effort, Guardian-denied commands, and repeated start notifications retaining the original model context. Attribution needs to reflect the step that invoked the command, even when model settings change before a background process finishes.  ## What changed  - Add `model_slug` and `reasoning_effort` to command execution and plugin measurement analytics. - Capture model context from resolved step settings and carry it through execution, approval, Guardian review, and plugin metrics collection. - Preserve the first command-start model context when subsequent start notifications arrive. - Keep the carried context out of serialized protocol items and generated schemas.  ## Testing  Extend analytics tests to cover model switches before invocation and during background execution, default reasoning effort, Guardian-denied commands, and repeated start notifications retaining the original model context. | [realtime\_conversation](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/realtime_conversation "realtime_conversation") | [realtime\_conversation](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/realtime_conversation "realtime_conversation") | [Persist realtime transcript tails before closure without inference (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/7d5f55bdad2d184019831f48a7c77d35af0fa1d5 "Persist realtime transcript tails before closure without inference (#50531)  ## Why  When `flush_transcript_tail_on_session_end` is enabled, remaining speech must be available in thread history as soon as realtime closure is reported, even while a model response is still running. Saving that transcript should not trigger another model request.  ## What changed  Record the transcript tail directly in model and client history, attaching it to the active turn or creating a completed history turn when idle. Flush persistence before emitting `RealtimeConversationClosed`, and keep realtime context active until recording finishes so the return to text instructions follow the transcript.  ## Testing  Extend core and app-server tests to cover immediate history visibility in paginated and legacy modes, idle and active turns, closure without extra inference, transcript reuse in subsequent text turns, and interruption after closure.  GitOrigin-RevId: ae323b711f22aa88db1432e24ad4bb3eb3f08f6b") [#…](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/50531) | 1 hour agoOct 3, 2026 |
| [realtime\_history](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/realtime_history "realtime_history") | [realtime\_history](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/realtime_history "realtime_history") | [Record realtime conversation history in Core (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/64c9cde458b6b1c1d332577425b927f0436ab40b "Record realtime conversation history in Core (#41924)  ## Why  Realtime history should be recorded consistently for every Core host, including when no app-server event listener is attached.  ## What changed  - Move transcript segmentation, session boundaries, and backing-agent artifact   promotion into Core for paginated threads. - Persist canonical realtime items through the thread store in event order and   emit dedicated history lifecycle events for hosts to present. - Translate those Core events into the existing app-server realtime item   notifications without app-server persisting the items a second time.  ## Testing  - Cover Core-only persistence across repeated sessions, ephemeral sessions,   accepted and rejected steering, typed input ordering, and artifact promotion. - Verify app-server notifications correspond to the persisted timeline. | [thread\_manager](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/thread_manager "thread_manager") | [thread\_manager](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/thread_manager "thread_manager") | [Prevent shared instruction providers from delegating to themselves (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/9d53dd91b5b5411dbfa8366fe7fd352ab3b1ddec "Prevent shared instruction providers from delegating to themselves (#50093)  ## Why  Reloading an unloaded root from a surviving child can supply the shared instruction wrapper already registered for that root. Replacing its underlying provider with that same wrapper makes instruction loading recurse into itself.  ## What changed  Reuse the existing wrapper without replacing its provider when both are the same `Arc`. Continue to accept distinct replacement providers.  ## Testing  Extend regression coverage to reload the root from a surviving child and verify that shared instructions still update. Check wrapper reuse, replacement with a distinct shared provider, and freezing the shared snapshot when switching to a provider that does not share with subagents.  GitOrigin-RevId: 7b6b2f9031f206e203f6fa14e1478a36b305ec39") [#…](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/50093) | yesterdayOct 1, 2026 |
| [tools](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/tools "tools") | [tools](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/main/codex-rs/core/src/tools "tools") | [Keep MCP resource helpers available in code mode (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/55b6f282a810c3146a1f79c7c2e6e919cc0aa974 "Keep MCP resource helpers available in code mode (#50546)  ## Why  In `CodeModeOnly`, MCP resource helper definitions changed when the first server was added or the last server was removed.  ## What changed  Always register `list_mcp_resources`, `list_mcp_resource_templates`, and `read_mcp_resource` in `CodeModeOnly`, even without configured MCP servers. This keeps their definitions stable across server configuration changes.  ## Testing  Add a regression scenario that calls all three helpers before adding a server, with a server registered, and after removing it. Assert identical tool definitions across requests, empty resource and template lists, and the expected error when reading from an unknown server.  GitOrigin-RevId: c5777efac250b83dd4bb1c9100a6b6169470556f") [#50546](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/50546) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/55b6f282a810c3146a1f79c7c2e6e919cc0aa974 "Keep MCP resource helpers available in code mode (#50546)  ## Why  In `CodeModeOnly`, MCP resource helper definitions changed when the first server was added or the last server was removed.  ## What changed  Always register `list_mcp_resources`, `list_mcp_resource_templates`, and `read_mcp_resource` in `CodeModeOnly`, even without configured MCP servers. This keeps their definitions stable across server configuration changes.  ## Testing  Add a regression scenario that calls all three helpers before adding a server, with a server registered, and after removing it. Assert identical tool definitions across requests, empty resource and template lists, and the expected error when reading from an unknown server.  GitOrigin-RevId: c5777efac250b83dd4bb1c9100a6b6169470556f") | 9 minutes agoOct 3, 2026 |

| [agent\_communication.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/agent_communication.rs "agent_communication.rs") | [agent\_communication.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/agent_communication.rs "agent_communication.rs") | [Preserve queued agent mail across session eviction (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/960e878df4bf87b9d7fa0849f14561d7cdcca942 "Preserve queued agent mail across session eviction (#50087)  ## Why  Unread queue-only messages prevented idle agents from unloading, and sending a message to an evicted agent reloaded its session. Pending mail should not require an idle recipient to occupy a loaded thread slot.  ## What changed  - Retain queue-only mail in an in-memory runtime mailbox when a session unloads.   Queue new messages for locally evicted agents without reloading them or   starting a turn. - Add `AgentControl::take_mailbox` and `AgentControl::watch_mailbox` so sessions   can consume retained mail and react to host mailbox notifications. - Preserve message order across eviction, reload, and subsequent follow-ups.   Keep agents with outstanding durable sleep or turn-triggering mail loaded,   and discard retained mail when an agent is removed or its tree shuts down.  ## Testing  Add integration coverage for retaining mail across eviction, sending while unloaded, and ordering messages around a follow-up after reload. Add a host controller test verifying that mailbox notifications wake `wait_agent` and that the message is delivered exactly once. Pending mail should not require an idle recipient to occupy a loaded thread slot.  ## What changed  - Retain queue-only mail in an in-memory runtime mailbox when a session unloads. Keep agents with outstanding durable sleep or turn-triggering mail loaded,   and discard retained mail when an agent is removed or its tree shuts down.  ## Testing  Add integration coverage for retaining mail across eviction, sending while unloaded, and ordering messages around a follow-up after reload. | [agent\_message\_board.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/agent_message_board.rs "agent_message_board.rs") | [agent\_message\_board.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/agent_message_board.rs "agent_message_board.rs") | [Deliver remote message board notifications to active turns (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/49be2c7ab029434dc9545cf85a7a41fd5d3beb1a "Deliver remote message board notifications to active turns (#49686)  ## What changed  - Start a turn-owned notification receiver using the same remote board client and session identity as the board tools. Deliver post previews as agent messages and cancel the receiver when the turn stops, aborts, or errors. - Wait for the initial connection attempt before inference, then retry failed or disconnected streams without waking idle agents. - Skip malformed notices, mismatched recipients or turns, and oversized previews. Reject delivery to stale or canceled turns and bound remote notices to a 1 KiB context budget.  ## Testing  Extend remote board tests to cover interrupted setup, connection failures, reconnection, invalid and stale notices, preview delivery into model context, and receiver shutdown after turn completion. | [agents\_md.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/agents_md.rs "agents_md.rs") | [agents\_md.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/agents_md.rs "agents_md.rs") | [Reject paths in project documentation fallback filenames (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/50d77959bf927293c4b5ddcca81d05331ae582ea "Reject paths in project documentation fallback filenames (#45865)  ## Why  Probing Windows network paths can send ambient credentials, even during metadata checks. Entries in `project_doc_fallback_filenames` must be validated before filesystem probes.  ## What changed  Ignore entries containing path syntax according to the executor's OS, rather than the host's. Reject `.` and `..`, slashes, and NUL characters on all executors; also reject backslashes and colons on Windows. Log a warning for rejected entries and continue considering valid filenames.  ## Testing  Add tests that verify invalid entries never reach metadata probes under POSIX and Windows path conventions, preserve backslashes and colons in POSIX filenames, and confirm valid fallback instructions still load alongside invalid entries. Entries in `project_doc_fallback_filenames` must be validated before filesystem probes.  ## What changed  Ignore entries containing path syntax according to the executor's OS, rather than the host's. Reject `.` and `..`, slashes, and NUL characters on all executors; also reject backslashes and colons on Windows. Log a warning for rejected entries and continue considering valid filenames.  ## Testing  Add tests that verify invalid entries never reach metadata probes under POSIX and Windows path conventions, preserve backslashes and colons in POSIX filenames, and confirm valid fallback instructions still load alongside invalid entries. | [agents\_md\_manager.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/agents_md_manager.rs "agents_md_manager.rs") | [agents\_md\_manager.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/agents_md_manager.rs "agents_md_manager.rs") | [Allow thread instruction providers to share updates with subagents (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/e24ac448b37bc6a29e6f2ce8dad61ddaaabf09dc "Allow thread instruction providers to share updates with subagents (#46577)  ## Why  Subagents inherit applied instruction snapshots, so later provider updates do not reach running descendants. Hosts need an explicit way to share current thread instructions across an agent tree.  ## What changed  - Add `ThreadInstructionsProvider::share_with_subagents()`, defaulting to `false`. Opted-in descendants refresh instructions at their next model-request boundary. - Retain shared providers across root unloads and descendant reloads. Let a resumed root replace the provider for surviving descendants, or preserve their last shared instructions when it supplies a non-sharing provider. - Keep providers scoped to their root tree and preserve snapshot-only instructions for root forks and isolated sessions, including Guardian reviews.  ## Testing  Add coverage for child and grandchild instruction updates and clearing, provider replacement after root restart, lazy descendant reloads, default snapshot inheritance, and isolation between roots and Guardian sessions.  GitOrigin-RevId: 44c9fb7fe9ae1f96156bdf3c47ab6c4a81bd65e9") [#…](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/46577) | 2 weeks agoSep 19, 2026 |

| [apply\_patch.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/apply_patch.rs "apply_patch.rs") | [apply\_patch.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/apply_patch.rs "apply_patch.rs") | [Fix macOS system-alias matching in patch permission checks (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/6f51c65958ebdd5a57708e012d045f172fc9aab4 "Fix macOS system-alias matching in patch permission checks (#47879)  ## Why  Local `apply_patch` permission checks can treat a macOS system alias and its canonical spelling as different locations, sending writes covered by temporary-directory grants for unnecessary approval.  ## What changed  - Normalize trusted top-level aliases in local policy roots and patch targets, reusing a prepared matcher for permission requests and patch safety checks. - Share alias normalization with Seatbelt and the macOS executor sandbox while preserving mutable symlinks and missing descendants. - Preserve read-only and deny precedence, protected metadata restrictions, and remote URI matching. Propagate normalization failures from fallible permission checks.  ## Testing  Add macOS regressions for multi-file patches with missing parent directories through both direct `apply_patch` calls and shell interception, asserting automatic approval and file contents. Add unit coverage for alias restriction precedence, mutable symlink preservation, missing descendants, and remote permission isolation. | [attestation.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/attestation.rs "attestation.rs") | [attestation.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/attestation.rs "attestation.rs") | [\[codex\] request desktop attestation from app (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/5f4d0ec343d807f6932e6bdc5785dc5a127ac409 "[codex] request desktop attestation from app (#20619)  ## Summary  TL;DR: teaches `codex-rs` / app-server to request a desktop-provided attestation token and attach it as `x-oai-attestation` on the scoped ChatGPT Codex request paths.  ![DeviceCheck attestation interface](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/codex/dev/jm/devicecheck-diagram-assets/pr-assets/devicecheck-attestation-interface.png)  ## Details  This PR teaches the Codex app-server runtime how to request and attach an attestation token. It does not generate DeviceCheck tokens directly; instead, it relies on the connected desktop app to advertise that it can generate attestation and then asks that app for a fresh header value when needed.  The flow is:  1. The Codex desktop app connects to app-server. 2. During `initialize`, the app can advertise that it supports `requestAttestation`. 3. Before app-server calls selected ChatGPT Codex endpoints, it sends the internal server request `attestation/generate` to the app. 4. app-server receives a pre-encoded header value back. 5. app-server forwards that value as `x-oai-attestation` on the scoped outbound requests.  The code in this repo is mostly protocol and runtime plumbing: it adds the app-server request/response shape, introduces an attestation provider in core, wires that provider into Responses / compaction / realtime setup paths, and covers the intended scoping with tests. The signed macOS DeviceCheck generation remains owned by the desktop app PR.  ## Related PR  - Codex desktop app implementation: https://github.com/openai/openai/pull/878649  ## Validation  <details> <summary>Tests run</summary>  ```sh cargo test -p codex-app-server-protocol cargo test -p codex-core attestation --lib cargo test -p codex-app-server --lib attestation ```  Also ran:  ```sh just fix -p codex-core just fix -p codex-app-server just fix -p codex-app-server-protocol just fmt just write-app-server-schema ```  </details>  <details> <summary>E2E DeviceCheck validation</summary>  First validated the signed desktop app boundary directly: launched a packaged signed `Codex.app`, sent `attestation/generate`, decoded the returned `v1.` attestation header, and validated the extracted DeviceCheck token with `personal/jm/verify_devicecheck_token.py` using bundle ID `com.openai.codex`. Apple returned `status_code: 200` and `is_ok: true`.  Then ran the fuller app + app-server flow. The packaged `Codex.app` launched a current-branch app-server via `CODEX_CLI_PATH`, and a local MITM proxy intercepted outbound `chatgpt.com` traffic. The app-server requested `attestation/generate` from the real Electron app process, and the intercepted `/backend-api/codex/responses` traffic included `x-oai-attestation` on both routes:  ```text GET  /backend-api/codex/responses  Upgrade: websocket  x-oai-attestation: present POST /backend-api/codex/responses  Upgrade: none       x-oai-attestation: present ```  The captured header decoded to a DeviceCheck token that also validated with Apple for `com.openai.codex` (`status_code: 200`, `is_ok: true`, team `2DC432GLL2`).  </details>  ---------  Co-authored-by: Codex <noreply@openai.com>") [#20619](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/20619) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/5f4d0ec343d807f6932e6bdc5785dc5a127ac409 "[codex] request desktop attestation from app (#20619)  ## Summary  TL;DR: teaches `codex-rs` / app-server to request a desktop-provided attestation token and attach it as `x-oai-attestation` on the scoped ChatGPT Codex request paths.  ![DeviceCheck attestation interface](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/codex/dev/jm/devicecheck-diagram-assets/pr-assets/devicecheck-attestation-interface.png)  ## Details  This PR teaches the Codex app-server runtime how to request and attach an attestation token. It does not generate DeviceCheck tokens directly; instead, it relies on the connected desktop app to advertise that it can generate attestation and then asks that app for a fresh header value when needed.  The flow is:  1. The Codex desktop app connects to app-server. 2. During `initialize`, the app can advertise that it supports `requestAttestation`. 3. Before app-server calls selected ChatGPT Codex endpoints, it sends the internal server request `attestation/generate` to the app. 4. app-server receives a pre-encoded header value back. 5. app-server forwards that value as `x-oai-attestation` on the scoped outbound requests.  The code in this repo is mostly protocol and runtime plumbing: it adds the app-server request/response shape, introduces an attestation provider in core, wires that provider into Responses / compaction / realtime setup paths, and covers the intended scoping with tests. The signed macOS DeviceCheck generation remains owned by the desktop app PR.  ## Related PR  - Codex desktop app implementation: https://github.com/openai/openai/pull/878649  ## Validation  <details> <summary>Tests run</summary>  ```sh cargo test -p codex-app-server-protocol cargo test -p codex-core attestation --lib cargo test -p codex-app-server --lib attestation ```  Also ran:  ```sh just fix -p codex-core just fix -p codex-app-server just fix -p codex-app-server-protocol just fmt just write-app-server-schema ```  </details>  <details> <summary>E2E DeviceCheck validation</summary>  First validated the signed desktop app boundary directly: launched a packaged signed `Codex.app`, sent `attestation/generate`, decoded the returned `v1.` attestation header, and validated the extracted DeviceCheck token with `personal/jm/verify_devicecheck_token.py` using bundle ID `com.openai.codex`. Apple returned `status_code: 200` and `is_ok: true`.  Then ran the fuller app + app-server flow. The packaged `Codex.app` launched a current-branch app-server via `CODEX_CLI_PATH`, and a local MITM proxy intercepted outbound `chatgpt.com` traffic. The app-server requested `attestation/generate` from the real Electron app process, and the intercepted `/backend-api/codex/responses` traffic included `x-oai-attestation` on both routes:  ```text GET  /backend-api/codex/responses  Upgrade: websocket  x-oai-attestation: present POST /backend-api/codex/responses  Upgrade: none       x-oai-attestation: present ```  The captured header decoded to a DeviceCheck token that also validated with Apple for `com.openai.codex` (`status_code: 200`, `is_ok: true`, team `2DC432GLL2`).  </details>  ---------  Co-authored-by: Codex <noreply@openai.com>") | 5 months agoMay 8, 2026 |
| [client.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/client.rs "client.rs") | [client.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/client.rs "client.rs") | [Send incremental tool catalog updates in Responses Lite (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/6326163b9abd7802c0e57be4e326e5f898bbba75 "Send incremental tool catalog updates in Responses Lite (#50540)  ## What changed  When `IncrementalTools` is enabled for Responses Lite, track tool definitions in world state and conversation history. Send the initial catalog once, then append only added or changed definitions while preserving earlier request history. Emit developer notices for removed tools and namespace instruction changes.  Use history-supplied definitions for normal requests and remote compaction, and omit empty `additional_tools` prefixes. Keep the existing tool delivery behavior for the Responses API and when the feature is disabled.  ## Testing  Add unit and snapshot coverage for catalog and namespace transitions, empty catalogs, and stable hashing across JSON object key order. Add a request-history scenario covering unchanged tools, a model switch that changes one definition, persisted catalog hashes, and Responses API behavior. | [client\_tool\_metadata.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/client_tool_metadata.rs "client_tool_metadata.rs") | [client\_tool\_metadata.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/client_tool_metadata.rs "client_tool_metadata.rs") | [Bound tool-call observations to the outgoing Responses message budget (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/dbb875d23cfd1a327cf6b51d7b50dad4b4601268 "Bound tool-call observations to the outgoing Responses message budget (#47957)  ## Why  Removing tool-result metadata alone can leave requests above the 15 MiB message budget when recorded tool calls and arguments occupy the remaining space. If trimming loses calls or arguments from a Code Mode cell, later waits must not report its call inventory as complete.  ## What changed  - Bound all optional tool-call observations against the serialized HTTP or WebSocket message size, preserving ordinary tool outputs and continuation history. - Shed generic result metadata, sources, and recorded arguments before resource-access evidence, retaining tool names and smaller metadata where possible. - Invalidate completeness across affected cell outputs and later waits when the outgoing copy loses calls or arguments. - Preserve size-limit omission markers for direct tool-result metadata when they fit, while continuing to exclude that metadata from custom providers.  ## Testing  Add regression coverage for HTTP and WebSocket message budgets, unchanged tool results and history, resource-evidence retention, direct-call omission markers, and Code Mode waits after argument truncation.  GitOrigin-RevId: 538dc9d8c11b0830ec70327422f98a1803122e85") […](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/47957) | last weekSep 24, 2026 |

GitOrigin-RevId: 538dc9d8c11b0830ec70327422f98a1803122e85") […](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/47957) | last weekSep 24, 2026 |
| [codex\_delegate.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/codex_delegate.rs "codex_delegate.rs") | [codex\_delegate.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/codex_delegate.rs "codex_delegate.rs") | [Capture host-supplied extension data for each turn (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/408f48ce0c87827dd556953a9d413323a139e38c "Capture host-supplied extension data for each turn (#49846)  ## What changed  Add `turn_extension_init` to thread startup and settings, and expose `WithTurnExtensionData` for turn input, recovery, and settings requests. Accept or reject data updates together with the request that supplies them.  Each new turn captures the configured data, including automatically started turns. Accepted steering updates the data for future turns while preserving the running turn's capture. Plain requests preserve the configured data; an empty initializer clears it. The data is not persisted and must be supplied again on cold resume.  Expose `current_turn_extension_data` to retrieve typed data only when the requested turn is still running and has not been cancelled.  ## Testing  Add an integration test covering initial capture, accepted and rejected steering, inheritance by an automatic turn, clearing future data without changing the active capture, and rejection of lookups for an earlier turn. | [codex\_thread.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/codex_thread.rs "codex_thread.rs") | [codex\_thread.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/codex_thread.rs "codex_thread.rs") | [Expose the model selected for a running turn's next step (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/4f99920b8cbb9dc694140719a0ea0f175c250a5b "Expose the model selected for a running turn's next step (#50128)  ## What changed  Add `CodexThread::current_turn_model` to return the model slug selected for the named running turn's next step, independently of settings for future turns. Return `None` when the turn ID does not match, the task is cancelled, or the turn is no longer running.  ## Testing  Extend the step-settings test to verify that the accessor ignores model changes for future turns, reflects active-turn model updates, and returns `None` for a mismatched ID or a completed turn. | [command\_canonicalization.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/command_canonicalization.rs "command_canonicalization.rs") | [command\_canonicalization.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/command_canonicalization.rs "command_canonicalization.rs") | [core: remove cross-crate re-exports from lib.rs (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/aa2403e2eb4126a6bebb50b9db8d943510bd37b9 "core: remove cross-crate re-exports from lib.rs (#16512)  ## Why  `codex-core` was re-exporting APIs owned by sibling `codex-*` crates, which made downstream crates depend on `codex-core` as a proxy module instead of the actual owner crate.  Removing those forwards makes crate boundaries explicit and lets leaf crates drop unnecessary `codex-core` dependencies. In this PR, this reduces the dependency on `codex-core` to `codex-login` in the following files:  ``` codex-rs/backend-client/Cargo.toml codex-rs/mcp-server/tests/common/Cargo.toml ```  ## What  - Remove `codex-rs/core/src/lib.rs` re-exports for symbols owned by `codex-login`, `codex-mcp`, `codex-rollout`, `codex-analytics`, `codex-protocol`, `codex-shell-command`, `codex-sandboxing`, `codex-tools`, and `codex-utils-path`. - Delete the `default_client` forwarding shim in `codex-rs/core`. - Update in-crate and downstream callsites to import directly from the owning `codex-*` crate. - Add direct Cargo dependencies where callsites now target the owner crate, and remove `codex-core` from `codex-rs/backend-client`.") [#16512](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/16512) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/aa2403e2eb4126a6bebb50b9db8d943510bd37b9 "core: remove cross-crate re-exports from lib.rs (#16512)  ## Why  `codex-core` was re-exporting APIs owned by sibling `codex-*` crates, which made downstream crates depend on `codex-core` as a proxy module instead of the actual owner crate.  Removing those forwards makes crate boundaries explicit and lets leaf crates drop unnecessary `codex-core` dependencies. In this PR, this reduces the dependency on `codex-core` to `codex-login` in the following files:  ``` codex-rs/backend-client/Cargo.toml codex-rs/mcp-server/tests/common/Cargo.toml ```  ## What  - Remove `codex-rs/core/src/lib.rs` re-exports for symbols owned by `codex-login`, `codex-mcp`, `codex-rollout`, `codex-analytics`, `codex-protocol`, `codex-shell-command`, `codex-sandboxing`, `codex-tools`, and `codex-utils-path`. - Delete the `default_client` forwarding shim in `codex-rs/core`. - Update in-crate and downstream callsites to import directly from the owning `codex-*` crate. - Add direct Cargo dependencies where callsites now target the owner crate, and remove `codex-core` from `codex-rs/backend-client`.") | 7 months agoApr 2, 2026 |
| [command\_canonicalization\_tests.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/command_canonicalization_tests.rs "command_canonicalization_tests.rs") | [command\_canonicalization\_tests.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/command_canonicalization_tests.rs "command_canonicalization_tests.rs") | [fix: move inline codex-rs/core unit tests into sibling files (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/0c8a36676ac3e0659f13662de1e8b78e38204e9d "fix: move inline codex-rs/core unit tests into sibling files (#14444)  ## Why PR #13783 moved the `codex.rs` unit tests into `codex_tests.rs`. This applies the same extraction pattern across the rest of `codex-rs/core` so the production modules stay focused on runtime code instead of large inline test blocks.  Keeping the tests in sibling files also makes follow-up edits easier to review because product changes no longer have to share a file with hundreds or thousands of lines of test scaffolding.  ## What changed - replaced each inline `mod tests { ... }` in `codex-rs/core/src/**` with a path-based module declaration - moved each extracted unit test module into a sibling `*_tests.rs` file, using `mod_tests.rs` for `mod.rs` modules - preserved the existing `cfg(...)` guards and module-local structure so the refactor remains structural rather than behavioral  ## Testing - `cargo test -p codex-core --lib` (`1653 passed; 0 failed; 5 ignored`) - `just fix -p codex-core` - `cargo fmt --check` - `cargo shear`") [#14444](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/14444) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/0c8a36676ac3e0659f13662de1e8b78e38204e9d "fix: move inline codex-rs/core unit tests into sibling files (#14444)  ## Why PR #13783 moved the `codex.rs` unit tests into `codex_tests.rs`. This applies the same extraction pattern across the rest of `codex-rs/core` so the production modules stay focused on runtime code instead of large inline test blocks.  Keeping the tests in sibling files also makes follow-up edits easier to review because product changes no longer have to share a file with hundreds or thousands of lines of test scaffolding.  ## What changed - replaced each inline `mod tests { ... }` in `codex-rs/core/src/**` with a path-based module declaration - moved each extracted unit test module into a sibling `*_tests.rs` file, using `mod_tests.rs` for `mod.rs` modules - preserved the existing `cfg(...)` guards and module-local structure so the refactor remains structural rather than behavioral  ## Testing - `cargo test -p codex-core --lib` (`1653 passed; 0 failed; 5 ignored`) - `just fix -p codex-core` - `cargo fmt --check` - `cargo shear`") | 7 months agoMar 12, 2026 |
| [compact.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact.rs "compact.rs") | [compact.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact.rs "compact.rs") | [Persist world-state snapshots alongside rendered context (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/b44ca872b1059c65f0eb6a7534e9e8ae55df54a8 "Persist world-state snapshots alongside rendered context (#49847)  ## What changed  Return snapshots and model-visible fragments together from world-state rendering, and use those snapshots for history baselines, step updates, compaction, and new context windows.  Update `WorldStateSectionContribution` so its renderer returns an optional replacement snapshot and an optional fragment. A missing snapshot preserves stored section state, even when retained history requires a fresh render; a null snapshot removes the section. Derive step patches from the history baseline rather than a separately captured live world state.  Adapt skills and Git attribution contributors to the new contract.  ## Testing  Add coverage for skipped snapshot updates, explicit section removal, changed-state patch replay, unchanged-state deduplication, and matching live and persisted checkpoints when starting a new context window.  GitOrigin-RevId: af71606a278358ae937853cb76e8a019a501408e") [#49847](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/49847) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/b44ca872b1059c65f0eb6a7534e9e8ae55df54a8 "Persist world-state snapshots alongside rendered context (#49847)  ## What changed  Return snapshots and model-visible fragments together from world-state rendering, and use those snapshots for history baselines, step updates, compaction, and new context windows.  Update `WorldStateSectionContribution` so its renderer returns an optional replacement snapshot and an optional fragment. A missing snapshot preserves stored section state, even when retained history requires a fresh render; a null snapshot removes the section. Derive step patches from the history baseline rather than a separately captured live world state.  Adapt skills and Git attribution contributors to the new contract.  ## Testing  Add coverage for skipped snapshot updates, explicit section removal, changed-state patch replay, unchanged-state deduplication, and matching live and persisted checkpoints when starting a new context window.  GitOrigin-RevId: af71606a278358ae937853cb76e8a019a501408e") | 2 days agoOct 1, 2026 |
| [compact\_model\_fallback.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_model_fallback.rs "compact_model_fallback.rs") | [compact\_model\_fallback.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_model_fallback.rs "compact_model_fallback.rs") | [Broaden compaction fallback to the current model (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/5492c2b06e9d3e1c09cf65e5923930a6b57b4e9e "Broaden compaction fallback to the current model (#46324)  ## Why  After a model switch, compaction with the previous model could fail after exhausting stream retries without falling back to the selected model.  ## What changed  Allow compaction to fall back to the current model for all errors except `TurnAborted`, `Interrupted`, and `SessionBudgetExceeded`.  ## Testing  Add a regression test that exhausts the previous model's compaction stream retries, then verifies that fallback compaction and turn sampling use the selected model.  GitOrigin-RevId: 9c9b7ccbb206d19f1ae750a32636fc9acaacfb2b") [#46324](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/46324) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/5492c2b06e9d3e1c09cf65e5923930a6b57b4e9e "Broaden compaction fallback to the current model (#46324)  ## Why  After a model switch, compaction with the previous model could fail after exhausting stream retries without falling back to the selected model.  ## What changed  Allow compaction to fall back to the current model for all errors except `TurnAborted`, `Interrupted`, and `SessionBudgetExceeded`.  ## Testing  Add a regression test that exhausts the previous model's compaction stream retries, then verifies that fallback compaction and turn sampling use the selected model.  GitOrigin-RevId: 9c9b7ccbb206d19f1ae750a32636fc9acaacfb2b") | 2 weeks agoSep 17, 2026 |

| [compact\_remote\_v2.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_remote_v2.rs "compact_remote_v2.rs") | [compact\_remote\_v2.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_remote_v2.rs "compact_remote_v2.rs") | [Persist explicit user goal edits in model history (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/de020167989fde4937204a064ea2fedd54f3c931 "Persist explicit user goal edits in model history (#49598)  ## Why  User goal edits and clears need to reach the model as user instructions, including when a thread is unloaded or history is being compacted. Automatic goal lifecycle updates must not supply user authorization.  ## What changed  - Add optional `origin` values of `user` and `automatic` to `thread/goal/set` and `thread/goal/clear`, and mark TUI goal requests as user actions. Omitted origins do not supply user authorization. - Persist explicit user objective, status, and clear instructions before changing goal state. Recording failures leave goal state unchanged. - Coordinate goal edits with thread resume, reload stored history under writer ownership, and preserve edits accepted after the compaction input snapshot. - Update generated protocol schemas and TypeScript and Python bindings.  ## Testing  Add coverage for user edits and clears on loaded and unloaded threads, concurrent path-based resume, exclusion of automatic goals from user instructions, preservation of edits during compaction, and recording failures that leave goal state unchanged.  GitOrigin-RevId: d779d7c678a0c32a6ef76975dbe5b1400d1df245") [#49598](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/49598) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/de020167989fde4937204a064ea2fedd54f3c931 "Persist explicit user goal edits in model history (#49598)  ## Why  User goal edits and clears need to reach the model as user instructions, including when a thread is unloaded or history is being compacted. Automatic goal lifecycle updates must not supply user authorization.  ## What changed  - Add optional `origin` values of `user` and `automatic` to `thread/goal/set` and `thread/goal/clear`, and mark TUI goal requests as user actions. Omitted origins do not supply user authorization. - Persist explicit user objective, status, and clear instructions before changing goal state. Recording failures leave goal state unchanged. - Coordinate goal edits with thread resume, reload stored history under writer ownership, and preserve edits accepted after the compaction input snapshot. - Update generated protocol schemas and TypeScript and Python bindings.  ## Testing  Add coverage for user edits and clears on loaded and unloaded threads, concurrent path-based resume, exclusion of automatic goals from user instructions, preservation of edits during compaction, and recording failures that leave goal state unchanged. | [compact\_remote\_v2\_attempt.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_remote_v2_attempt.rs "compact_remote_v2_attempt.rs") | [compact\_remote\_v2\_attempt.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_remote_v2_attempt.rs "compact_remote_v2_attempt.rs") | [Send incremental tool catalog updates in Responses Lite (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/6326163b9abd7802c0e57be4e326e5f898bbba75 "Send incremental tool catalog updates in Responses Lite (#50540)  ## What changed  When `IncrementalTools` is enabled for Responses Lite, track tool definitions in world state and conversation history. Send the initial catalog once, then append only added or changed definitions while preserving earlier request history. Emit developer notices for removed tools and namespace instruction changes.  Use history-supplied definitions for normal requests and remote compaction, and omit empty `additional_tools` prefixes. Keep the existing tool delivery behavior for the Responses API and when the feature is disabled.  ## Testing  Add unit and snapshot coverage for catalog and namespace transitions, empty catalogs, and stable hashing across JSON object key order. Add a request-history scenario covering unchanged tools, a model switch that changes one definition, persisted catalog hashes, and Responses API behavior. | [compact\_remote\_v2\_image\_budget\_tests.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_remote_v2_image_budget_tests.rs "compact_remote_v2_image_budget_tests.rs") | [compact\_remote\_v2\_image\_budget\_tests.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_remote_v2_image_budget_tests.rs "compact_remote_v2_image_budget_tests.rs") | [Account for file images in context budgets and Guardian reviews (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/b974893c90048d147207ae9004489c02f3116426 "Account for file images in context budgets and Guardian reviews (#46072)  ## Why  File images contributed zero to history and compaction token estimates and were dropped from Guardian context. This undercounted context usage and omitted image evidence from reviews.  ## What changed  - Estimate file images using the fixed resized-image cost, or 10,000 patch tokens for `original` detail because file references do not expose dimensions. - Charge file images against remote compaction and Guardian request budgets. - Preserve file images from user messages and tool outputs through Guardian selection and delivery, and deduplicate review images by file ID. - Apply transcript image count and byte limits to both reference types, counting file ID bytes rather than referenced file contents.  ## Testing  Add coverage for file-image estimates, selection limits, delivery, and compaction retention. Extend Guardian tests to verify file-image evidence reaches reviews and uploaded original-detail images trigger reviewer-history compaction.  GitOrigin-RevId: ee024204dcbe16e277fea9347c6a4d7fc120e18d") [#46072](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/46072) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/b974893c90048d147207ae9004489c02f3116426 "Account for file images in context budgets and Guardian reviews (#46072)  ## Why  File images contributed zero to history and compaction token estimates and were dropped from Guardian context. This undercounted context usage and omitted image evidence from reviews.  ## What changed  - Estimate file images using the fixed resized-image cost, or 10,000 patch tokens for `original` detail because file references do not expose dimensions. - Charge file images against remote compaction and Guardian request budgets. - Preserve file images from user messages and tool outputs through Guardian selection and delivery, and deduplicate review images by file ID. - Apply transcript image count and byte limits to both reference types, counting file ID bytes rather than referenced file contents.  ## Testing  Add coverage for file-image estimates, selection limits, delivery, and compaction retention. Extend Guardian tests to verify file-image evidence reaches reviews and uploaded original-detail images trigger reviewer-history compaction. | [compact\_remote\_v2\_images.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_remote_v2_images.rs "compact_remote_v2_images.rs") | [compact\_remote\_v2\_images.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_remote_v2_images.rs "compact_remote_v2_images.rs") | [Deduplicate retained instructions across Guardian reviews (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/86be5320b068ef67b56348b02aa8c33706955da6 "Deduplicate retained instructions across Guardian reviews (#48060)  ## Why  Reused Guardian sessions already contain previously delivered instructions. Follow-up reviews can avoid repeating that evidence, but must deliver it again if it changes or compaction removes it.  ## What changed  - Track retained source identity, revision, and completeness in host-owned history metadata, preserving revisions during replay. - Omit retained instructions from synchronous review inputs only when the same complete revision remains in reviewer history. - Restore missing evidence after reviewer compaction, enforcing the request budget before persisting additions. Classify restored evidence as context rather than new user authorization. - Keep source-order labels stable across eviction and retain full delivery for legacy records without reliable provenance.  ## Testing  Add coverage for consecutive-review deduplication, changed revisions, missing or incomplete metadata, and restoring compacted evidence exactly once. Extend compaction and resume coverage to verify retained revisions.  GitOrigin-RevId: e90fca16cde0a0970da36dd873af2ca1dbaaf4d3") [#48060](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/48060) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/86be5320b068ef67b56348b02aa8c33706955da6 "Deduplicate retained instructions across Guardian reviews (#48060)  ## Why  Reused Guardian sessions already contain previously delivered instructions. Follow-up reviews can avoid repeating that evidence, but must deliver it again if it changes or compaction removes it.  ## What changed  - Track retained source identity, revision, and completeness in host-owned history metadata, preserving revisions during replay. - Omit retained instructions from synchronous review inputs only when the same complete revision remains in reviewer history. - Restore missing evidence after reviewer compaction, enforcing the request budget before persisting additions. Classify restored evidence as context rather than new user authorization. - Keep source-order labels stable across eviction and retain full delivery for legacy records without reliable provenance.  ## Testing  Add coverage for consecutive-review deduplication, changed revisions, missing or incomplete metadata, and restoring compacted evidence exactly once. Extend compaction and resume coverage to verify retained revisions.  GitOrigin-RevId: e90fca16cde0a0970da36dd873af2ca1dbaaf4d3") | last weekSep 25, 2026 |
| [compact\_tests.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_tests.rs "compact_tests.rs") | [compact\_tests.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_tests.rs "compact_tests.rs") | [Restrict enterprise MCP auth and fail closed on config refresh (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/af0d68a23681dee6206bbcb58f9da1ce912bc5f3 "Restrict enterprise MCP auth and fail closed on config refresh (#49260)  ## Why  Configuration reloads must honor current managed restrictions without granting new enterprise MCP authority to existing sessions or overwriting newer configuration with stale snapshots.  ## What changed  - Allow enterprise managed authentication only for configured MCP servers. Remove plugin `ema_auth` overlays from the schema and disable servers that still use them. - Refresh ordinary MCP settings while preserving session settings and initially admitted enterprise registrations. Disable enterprise MCP when its registration changes, policy revokes access, or configuration loading or resolution fails; require a new session to restore enterprise authority. - Publish refreshes only when their captured configuration is still current, with bounded retries in app-server reloads. Preserve updated managed restrictions even when a refresh is rejected. - Finish processing other thread refreshes before `config/mcpServer/reload` reports a rejection, and document that an error can accompany partially applied changes.  ## Testing  Add regression coverage for stale refreshes, bounded retries, managed-policy revocation, persistent enterprise disablement, ordinary MCP transport changes, and rejection of plugin enterprise authentication. Verify that denied enterprise servers receive no requests.  GitOrigin-RevId: 34429cd339c1ec152a2ee8697cb29f0cff5d9e5f") [#49260](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/49260) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/af0d68a23681dee6206bbcb58f9da1ce912bc5f3 "Restrict enterprise MCP auth and fail closed on config refresh (#49260)  ## Why  Configuration reloads must honor current managed restrictions without granting new enterprise MCP authority to existing sessions or overwriting newer configuration with stale snapshots.  ## What changed  - Allow enterprise managed authentication only for configured MCP servers. Remove plugin `ema_auth` overlays from the schema and disable servers that still use them. - Refresh ordinary MCP settings while preserving session settings and initially admitted enterprise registrations. Disable enterprise MCP when its registration changes, policy revokes access, or configuration loading or resolution fails; require a new session to restore enterprise authority. - Publish refreshes only when their captured configuration is still current, with bounded retries in app-server reloads. Preserve updated managed restrictions even when a refresh is rejected. - Finish processing other thread refreshes before `config/mcpServer/reload` reports a rejection, and document that an error can accompany partially applied changes.  ## Testing  Add regression coverage for stale refreshes, bounded retries, managed-policy revocation, persistent enterprise disablement, ordinary MCP transport changes, and rejection of plugin enterprise authentication. Verify that denied enterprise servers receive no requests.  GitOrigin-RevId: 34429cd339c1ec152a2ee8697cb29f0cff5d9e5f") | 4 days agoSep 29, 2026 |
| [compact\_token\_budget.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_token_budget.rs "compact_token_budget.rs") | [compact\_token\_budget.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/compact_token_budget.rs "compact_token_budget.rs") | [Preserve root turn attribution in turn-start events (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/196964ef10db326047c3e71fc568693cbd7c58a8 "Preserve root turn attribution in turn-start events (#44611)  ## Why  Persisted turn-start events lack the originating root turn ID, so they cannot directly associate child turns with the root turn that initiated them.  ## What changed  - Add optional `root_turn_id` to `TurnStartedEvent`. Use the inherited root turn ID when available, otherwise the turn's own ID. - Centralize turn-start emission for regular turns, shell commands, and manual compaction so they record attribution consistently. - Carry attribution into `ThreadHistoryTurnChange` when processing turn-start events and retain it through completion in the history builder. - Keep older records compatible by defaulting missing attribution to `None`.  ## Testing  Add coverage for root and child turn attribution, persistence when tool collisions fail a turn before sampling, history change propagation, and deserialization of older turn-start records. Use the inherited root turn ID when available, otherwise the turn's own ID. - Centralize turn-start emission for regular turns, shell commands, and manual compaction so they record attribution consistently. - Carry attribution into `ThreadHistoryTurnChange` when processing turn-start events and retain it through completion in the history builder. - Keep older records compatible by defaulting missing attribution to `None`.  ## Testing  Add coverage for root and child turn attribution, persistence when tool collisions fail a turn before sampling, history change propagation, and deserialization of older turn-start records.  GitOrigin-RevId: 54a80b828ca7d1c4c19f13d468c01d052e1b9130") | 3 weeks agoSep 10, 2026 |
| [connectors.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/connectors.rs "connectors.rs") | [connectors.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/connectors.rs "connectors.rs") | [Allow exec-server to proxy permitted private IPs upstream (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/b8d5e3f12e58fff942093457bf1350f9ceb695e9 "Allow exec-server to proxy permitted private IPs upstream (#48568)  ## Why  Private IP destinations always bypassed inherited upstream proxies, preventing their use for private networks reachable through an upstream VPN proxy.  ## What changed  - Add `codex exec-server --proxy-private-ips-via-upstream`, also configurable with `CODEX_EXEC_SERVER_PROXY_PRIVATE_IPS_VIA_UPSTREAM=true`. The setting defaults to disabled. - Allow permitted RFC 1918, carrier-grade NAT, and IPv6 unique-local destinations to use an applicable upstream proxy. Loopback and link-local destinations retain direct routing, and destination access policy still applies. - Keep connections direct when no valid upstream proxy applies or `allow_upstream_proxy=false`. Errors after selecting an upstream proxy do not trigger a direct retry. - Rename `ExecServerRuntimePaths` to `ExecServerRuntimeOptions` and carry the routing setting from executor startup into the managed network proxy.  ## Testing  Add routing coverage for private address ranges, special-use addresses, and public targets with the option enabled and disabled. Verify that HTTP and CONNECT requests still enforce destination allowlists and denylists, and update the CLI help snapshot.  GitOrigin-RevId: b7c9cc7da0e0f545694a6521b74c9b36b7b92769") [#48568](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/48568) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/b8d5e3f12e58fff942093457bf1350f9ceb695e9 "Allow exec-server to proxy permitted private IPs upstream (#48568)  ## Why  Private IP destinations always bypassed inherited upstream proxies, preventing their use for private networks reachable through an upstream VPN proxy.  ## What changed  - Add `codex exec-server --proxy-private-ips-via-upstream`, also configurable with `CODEX_EXEC_SERVER_PROXY_PRIVATE_IPS_VIA_UPSTREAM=true`. The setting defaults to disabled. - Allow permitted RFC 1918, carrier-grade NAT, and IPv6 unique-local destinations to use an applicable upstream proxy. Loopback and link-local destinations retain direct routing, and destination access policy still applies. - Keep connections direct when no valid upstream proxy applies or `allow_upstream_proxy=false`. Errors after selecting an upstream proxy do not trigger a direct retry. - Rename `ExecServerRuntimePaths` to `ExecServerRuntimeOptions` and carry the routing setting from executor startup into the managed network proxy.  ## Testing  Add routing coverage for private address ranges, special-use addresses, and public targets with the option enabled and disabled. Verify that HTTP and CONNECT requests still enforce destination allowlists and denylists, and update the CLI help snapshot.  GitOrigin-RevId: b7c9cc7da0e0f545694a6521b74c9b36b7b92769") | last weekSep 26, 2026 |

| [current\_time.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/current_time.rs "current_time.rs") | [current\_time.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/current_time.rs "current_time.rs") | [\[codex\] route sleep through time providers (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/f66d793a2d78287c8c28a5f41f39c58ac49bcc25 "[codex] route sleep through time providers (#29973)  ## Summary  - add a cancellable sleep operation to `TimeProvider` - route `clock.sleep` through the configured provider - extend the supported sleep duration to 12 hours - complete the sleep turn item before propagating provider failures  ## Why  This isolates the core clock abstraction needed by external clock integrations. Existing system and app-server behavior remains wall-clock based in this PR; the stacked follow-up supplies app-server sleeps from an external clock.") [#29973](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/29973) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/f66d793a2d78287c8c28a5f41f39c58ac49bcc25 "[codex] route sleep through time providers (#29973)  ## Summary  - add a cancellable sleep operation to `TimeProvider` - route `clock.sleep` through the configured provider - extend the supported sleep duration to 12 hours - complete the sleep turn item before propagating provider failures  ## Why  This isolates the core clock abstraction needed by external clock integrations. Existing system and app-server behavior remains wall-clock based in this PR; the stacked follow-up supplies app-server sleeps from an external clock.") | 4 months agoJun 25, 2026 |
| [cyber\_access\_program.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/cyber_access_program.rs "cyber_access_program.rs") | [cyber\_access\_program.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/cyber_access_program.rs "cyber_access_program.rs") | [Add per-turn Cyber access program selection to exec and the SDK (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/6b4daafdb445340e5af66f067ad4057e6ed9fd81 "Add per-turn Cyber access program selection to exec and the SDK (#49939)  ## Why  `codex exec` and the TypeScript SDK need a way to request a Cyber access program for an individual turn. Resuming with an API key can also leave compaction using a saved program that the current credentials cannot access.  ## What changed  - Add `--cyber-access-program` with `standard`, `daybreak_blue`, and `daybreak_red`, and expose the equivalent `cyberAccessProgram` turn option and `CyberAccessProgram` type in the TypeScript SDK. - Apply explicit selections to new, resumed, and prompted fork turns. Override automatic Daybreak selection for that turn without changing the saved choice. Reject the flag for review and forks without a prompt. - Filter Cyber programs by the built-in OpenAI provider when starting turns and restoring previous-turn settings for compaction. - Allow previous-model compaction fallback with API-key authentication, comparing both model and program so a program change can trigger a retry with the current pair even when the model is unchanged.  ## Testing  Add CLI help, argument parsing, exec, and SDK coverage for per-turn selection and unsupported commands. Extend core tests for API-key compaction retries, denied explicit selections, custom-provider filtering, remote compaction, and WebSocket response reuse.  GitOrigin-RevId: 586f25b621924f3bc509c9a39b39fe2020ff9cd2") [#49939](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/49939) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/6b4daafdb445340e5af66f067ad4057e6ed9fd81 "Add per-turn Cyber access program selection to exec and the SDK (#49939)  ## Why  `codex exec` and the TypeScript SDK need a way to request a Cyber access program for an individual turn. Resuming with an API key can also leave compaction using a saved program that the current credentials cannot access.  ## What changed  - Add `--cyber-access-program` with `standard`, `daybreak_blue`, and `daybreak_red`, and expose the equivalent `cyberAccessProgram` turn option and `CyberAccessProgram` type in the TypeScript SDK. - Apply explicit selections to new, resumed, and prompted fork turns. Override automatic Daybreak selection for that turn without changing the saved choice. Reject the flag for review and forks without a prompt. - Filter Cyber programs by the built-in OpenAI provider when starting turns and restoring previous-turn settings for compaction. - Allow previous-model compaction fallback with API-key authentication, comparing both model and program so a program change can trigger a retry with the current pair even when the model is unchanged.  ## Testing  Add CLI help, argument parsing, exec, and SDK coverage for per-turn selection and unsupported commands. Extend core tests for API-key compaction retries, denied explicit selections, custom-provider filtering, remote compaction, and WebSocket response reuse.  GitOrigin-RevId: 586f25b621924f3bc509c9a39b39fe2020ff9cd2") | 2 days agoOct 1, 2026 |
| [elicitation.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/elicitation.rs "elicitation.rs") | [elicitation.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/elicitation.rs "elicitation.rs") | [elicitations: Move to shared ElicitationService (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/84fe70c30e88a1828a510804341202bab629d507 "elicitations: Move to shared ElicitationService (#30627)  ## Why  Code-mode tool results could return to the model while an MCP elicitation was still waiting for user input. This differed from parallel tool calling and could let the model continue before the user resolved the request.  We need one session-level view of outstanding elicitations so tool runtimes can consistently hold results until every pending elicitation is resolved.    ## What changed  - Added a counted, session-owned ElicitationService with RAII registrations. - Registered both core-originated and server-originated MCP elicitations with the service. - Migrated out-of-band elicitation tracking and unified exec timeout pausing to the shared service. - Made code-mode functions.exec and functions.wait capture their runtime result normally, then hold it before returning while an elicitation is outstanding.   - Kept terminate: true immediate; only its result is held.   - Preserved model-visible wall time across the elicitation hold. - Kept the behavior session-scoped, with concurrent elicitations holding the pause until all registrations are released.") [#30627](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/30627) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/84fe70c30e88a1828a510804341202bab629d507 "elicitations: Move to shared ElicitationService (#30627)  ## Why  Code-mode tool results could return to the model while an MCP elicitation was still waiting for user input. This differed from parallel tool calling and could let the model continue before the user resolved the request. | [environment\_selection.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/environment_selection.rs "environment_selection.rs") | [environment\_selection.rs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/core/src/environment_selection.rs "environment_selection.rs") | [Preserve pending environments when spawning subagents (](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/3749d1eff7df4ac9a0e2d32099813ba56500b930 "Preserve pending environments when spawning subagents (#49075)  ## Why  Spawning a subagent before an environment was ready dropped the starting environment from the child's selections. The child needs to retain that environment and receive its configuration or failure when preparation finishes.  ## What changed  - Pass the spawning step's environment snapshot to children, preserving ready and starting environments and configuration ownership. - Propagate the original owner's first configuration result through descendants, applying each session's validation and leaving independently configured children unchanged. - Preserve pending configuration waits across executor retries. Later owner updates do not overwrite the child's inherited configuration.  ## Testing  Add regression coverage for fresh and forked subagents, readiness through turn-settings updates, failure propagation to children and grandchildren, child configuration precedence, and executor retries. Add a request snapshot showing a child using `wait_for_environment` before execution tools become available.  GitOrigin-RevId: a2afed740391f88d195596cfe28e418332b60d43") [#49075](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/49075) [)](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/3749d1eff7df4ac9a0e2d32099813ba56500b930 "Preserve pending environments when spawning subagents (#49075)  ## Why  Spawning a subagent before an environment was ready dropped the starting environment from the child's selections. The child needs to retain that environment and receive its configuration or failure when preparation finishes.  ## What changed  - Pass the spawning step's environment snapshot to children, preserving ready and starting environments and configuration ownership. - Propagate the original owner's first configuration result through descendants, applying each session's validation and leaving independently configured children unchanged. - Preserve pending configuration waits across executor retries. Later owner updates do not overwrite the child's inherited configuration.  ## Testing  Add regression coverage for fresh and forked subagents, readiness through turn-settings updates, failure propagation to children and grandchildren, child configuration precedence, and executor retries. Add a request snapshot showing a child using `wait_for_environment` before execution tools become available.  GitOrigin-RevId: a2afed740391f88d195596cfe28e418332b60d43") | 4 days agoSep 28, 2026 |
